Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Polycarp starts his own business. Tomorrow will be the first working day of his car repair shop. For now the car repair shop is very small and only one car can be repaired at a given time.

Polycarp is good at marketing, so he has already collected n requests from clients. The requests are numbered from 1 to n in order they came.

The i-th request is characterized by two values: s_{i} — the day when a client wants to start the repair of his car, d_{i} — duration (in days) to repair the car. The days are enumerated from 1, the first day is tomorrow, the second day is the day after tomorrow and so on.

Polycarp is making schedule by processing requests in the order from the first to the n-th request. He schedules the i-th request as follows:  If the car repair shop is idle for d_{i} days starting from s_{i} (s_{i}, s_{i} + 1, ..., s_{i} + d_{i} - 1), then these days are used to repair a car of the i-th client.  Otherwise, Polycarp finds the first day x (from 1 and further) that there are d_{i} subsequent days when no repair is scheduled starting from x. In other words he chooses the smallest positive x that all days x, x + 1, ..., x + d_{i} - 1 are not scheduled for repair of any car. So, the car of the i-th client will be repaired in the range [x, x + d_{i} - 1]. It is possible that the day x when repair is scheduled to start will be less than s_{i}. 

Given n requests, you are asked to help Polycarp schedule all of them according to the rules above.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 200) — the number of requests from clients.

The following n lines contain requests, one request per line. The i-th request is given as the pair of integers s_{i}, d_{i} (1 ≤ s_{i} ≤ 10^9, 1 ≤ d_{i} ≤ 5·10^6), where s_{i} is the preferred time to start repairing the i-th car, d_{i} is the number of days to repair the i-th car.

The requests should be processed in the order they are given in the input.


-----Output-----

Print n lines. The i-th line should contain two integers — the start day to repair the i-th car and the finish day to repair the i-th car.


-----Examples-----
Input
3
9 2
7 3
2 4

Output
9 10
1 3
4 7

Input
4
1000000000 1000000
1000000000 1000000
100000000 1000000
1000000000 1000000

Output
1000000000 1000999999
1 1000000
100000000 100999999
1000001 2000000

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. When scheduling:
   - If the shop is idle for `d_i` consecutive days starting from `s_i`, we use those days
   - Otherwise, we find the first day `x` where `d_i` consecutive days are free (from day `x` to `x + d_i - 1`)
4. We need to output the actual start and finish days for each request

## Key Insights

1. We need to keep track of all scheduled repair periods
2. For each request, we check if the preferred start date works
3. If not, we need to find the first available slot of duration `d_i`
4. We need to maintain a timeline of occupied days

## Approach

1. Maintain a list of intervals that are already occupied
2. For each request:
   - Check if the preferred start day `s_i` has `d_i` free consecutive days
   - If yes, schedule it there
   - If no, find the first available gap of size `d_i`
3. To find available gaps, we need to merge overlapping intervals and check gaps between them

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Store all intervals as (start, end) pairs
2. For each new request, check if preferred start is available
3. If not available, find the first gap that can accommodate the duration
4. Update the intervals list with the new schedule

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        
        # Preferred start day
        preferred_start = s
        duration = d
        
        # Check if preferred start day is available
        # We need d consecutive days from preferred_start
        available = True
        for start, end in intervals:
            # Check if there's overlap between preferred range and existing intervals
            start_pref = preferred_start
            end_pref = preferred_start + duration - 1
            
            # Check overlap with current interval
            if start <= end_pref and start_pref <= end:
                # Overlap exists, so preferred start is not available
                available = False
                break
        
        if available:
            # Preferred start is available, use it
            start_day = preferred_start
            end_day = preferred_start + duration - 1
        else:
            # Find the first available slot of duration d
            # First, merge all existing intervals to get gaps
            # Sort intervals by start
            sorted_intervals = sorted(intervals, key=lambda x: x[0])
            
            # Find gaps between intervals
            current_pos = 1
            
            # Check if there's a gap at the beginning (from day 1)
            # or between existing intervals
            found = False
            start_day = None
            
            # Check gap at beginning
            if sorted_intervals and sorted_intervals[0][0] > 1:
                # There's a gap from day 1 to sorted_intervals[0][0]
                gap_start = 1
                gap_end = sorted_intervals[0][0] - 1
                gap_size = gap_end - gap_start + 1
                
                if gap_size >= duration:
                    start_day = gap_start
                    found = True
            else:
                # Check gaps between existing intervals
                for i in range(len(sorted_intervals) - 1):
                    interval1 = sorted_intervals[i]
                    interval2 = sorted_intervals[i + 1]
                    
                    gap_start = interval1[1] + 1
                    gap_end = interval2[0] - 1
                    gap_size = gap_end - gap_start + 1
                    
                    if gap_size >= duration:
                        start_day = gap_start
                        found = True
                        break
                
                # If we didn't find a gap, we need to extend beyond the last interval
                if not found:
                    # Find the first gap after the last interval
                    last_end = sorted_intervals[-1][1] if sorted_intervals else 0
                    gap_start = last_end + 1
                    gap_size = float('inf')
                    
                    if gap_size >= duration:
                        start_day = gap_start
                        found = True
            
            # If we still haven't found a gap, start from day 1
            if not found:
                start_day = 1
            
            # Find the first x such that x to x+d-1 is free
            # We need to check gaps one by one
            start_day = find_first_available_start(intervals, duration)
        
        # If we couldn't find a gap, start from day 1 (shouldn't happen with proper logic)
        if start_day is None:
            start_day = 1
            
        end_day = start_day + duration - 1
        
        # Add the new interval
        intervals.append((start_day, end_day))
        
        print(start_day, end_day)

def find_first_available_start(intervals, duration):
    # Sort intervals by start
    sorted_intervals = sorted(intervals, key=lambda x: x[0])
    
    # Check if there's a gap at the beginning
    if sorted_intervals and sorted_intervals[0][0] > 1:
        gap_start = 1
        gap_end = sorted_intervals[0][0] - 1
        gap_size = gap_end - gap_start + 1
        if gap_size >= duration:
            return gap_start
    
    # Check gaps between intervals
    for i in range(len(sorted_intervals) - 1):
        interval1 = sorted_intervals[i]
        interval2 = sorted_intervals[i + 1]
        
        gap_start = interval1[1] + 1
        gap_end = interval2[0] - 1
        gap_size = gap_end - gap_start + 1
        
        if gap_size >= duration:
            return gap_start
    
    # Check gap after the last interval
    last_end = sorted_intervals[-1][1] if sorted_intervals else 0
    gap_start = last_end + 1
    # This gap is infinite, so we can use it
    return gap_start

# Let me rewrite with a cleaner approach
def main():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        
        # Check if preferred start is available
        start_day = s
        end_day = start_day + d - 1
        
        # Check if any existing interval overlaps with [start_day, end_day]
        overlaps = False
        for interval_start, interval_end in intervals:
            if interval_start <= end_day and start_day <= interval_end:
                overlaps = True
                break
        
        if not overlaps:
            # Preferred start is available
            print(start_day, end_day)
            intervals.append((start_day, end_day))
        else:
            # Find the first available slot of duration d
            # Create a list of all existing intervals
            existing = intervals[:]
            existing.sort()
            
            # Find the first free slot
            candidate_start = 1
            
            # Check if there's a gap at the beginning
            if existing and existing[0][0] > 1:
                gap_start = 1
                gap_end = existing[0][0] - 1
                if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                    candidate_start = gap_start
            
            # Check gaps between existing intervals
            for i in range(len(existing) - 1):
                interval1_end = existing[i][1]
                interval2_start = existing[i + 1][0]
                
                gap_start = interval1_end + 1
                gap_end = interval2_start - 1
                gap_size = gap_end - gap_start + 1
                
                if gap_size >= d:
                    candidate_start = gap_start
                    break
            
            # If no gap found, extend beyond the last interval
            if candidate_start == 1:
                last_end = existing[-1][1] if existing else 0
                candidate_start = last_end + 1
            
            # Make sure we have a gap of at least duration
            # The gap from candidate_start to candidate_start + d - 1 should be free
            # But we need to check overlaps in our candidate
            # Actually, our gap finding should guarantee this
            
            end_day = candidate_start + d - 1
            print(candidate_start, end_day)
            intervals.append((candidate_start, end_day))

main()

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. We process requests in the given order (1 to n)
4. For each request, we check if we can use the preferred start day `s_i` for `d_i` consecutive days
5. If the preferred period is available (no overlapping repairs), we use it
6. If not, we need to find the earliest possible start day `x` such that days `[x, x+d_i-1]` are free
7. We output the start and finish days for each request

## Key Insights

1. We need to track all scheduled repair periods to check availability
2. For each new request, we first check if we can use the preferred start day
3. If not, we need to find the first free slot of `d_i` consecutive days
4. We need to efficiently find gaps in the schedule

## Algorithm

1. Maintain a list of intervals that represent scheduled repairs
2. For each request:
   - Check if the preferred period `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]` is free (no overlapping intervals)
   - If free, schedule it there
   - If not free, find the earliest free slot of `d_i` consecutive days by scanning from day 1 onward
3. When finding a free slot, we need to check if there's a gap of at least `d_i` consecutive days

## Implementation Strategy

Since the constraints are reasonable (n ≤ 200, but s_i can be up to 10^9), we can't iterate over all possible days. Instead, we only need to check the gaps between existing intervals.

For each new request, we:
1. Check if the preferred day works by verifying no interval overlaps with `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]`
2. If not, we scan through all existing intervals to find gaps where we can place the new repair

The key is that we can only place the repair in gaps between intervals, or after the last interval.

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        start_day = s
        end_day = s + d - 1
        
        # Check if preferred start day works
        can_use_preferred = True
        
        # Check if the preferred period overlaps with any existing interval
        for interval_start, interval_end in intervals:
            # Check overlap: intervals overlap if they are not completely separated
            if interval_end >= start_day and start_day <= interval_end:
                # Overlap exists, can't use preferred start
                can_use_preferred = False
                break
        
        if can_use_preferred:
            # Use the preferred start day
            intervals.append((start_day, end_day))
            print(start_day, end_day)
        else:
            # Need to find earliest free slot of d consecutive days
            # Check gaps between existing intervals and after last interval
            new_start = None
            
            # Check gaps between existing intervals
            # First gap: before the first interval
            if intervals:
                if intervals[0][0] > 1:
                    # Gap from 1 to intervals[0][0]-1
                    gap_start = 1
                    gap_end = intervals[0][0] - 1
                    if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                        new_start = gap_start
                else:
                    # No gap before first interval
                    pass
                
                # Check gaps between consecutive intervals
                for i in range(len(intervals) - 1):
                    interval1_end = intervals[i][1]
                    interval2_start = intervals[i + 1][0]
                    gap_start = interval1_end + 1
                    gap_end = interval2_start - 1
                    if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                        new_start = gap_start
                        break
            
            # If no gap found in between, check after the last interval
            if new_start is None:
                last_end = intervals[-1][1] if intervals else 0
                new_start = last_end + 1
                
            # The new interval will be from new_start to new_start + d - 1
            new_end = new_start + d - 1
            intervals.append((new_start, new_end))
            print(new_start, new_end)

solve()
```

The i-th request is characterized by two values: s_{i} — the day when a client wants to start the repair of his car, d_{i} — duration (in days) to repair the car. The days are enumerated from 1, the first day is tomorrow, the second day is the day after tomorrow and so on.

Polycarp is making schedule by processing requests in the order from the first to the n-th request. He schedules the i-th request as follows:  If the car repair shop is idle for d_{i} days starting from s_{i} (s_{i}, s_{i} + 1, ..., s_{i} + d_{i} - 1), then these days are used to repair a car of the i-th client.  Otherwise, Polycarp finds the first day x (from 1 and further) that there are d_{i} subsequent days when no repair is scheduled starting from x. In other words he chooses the smallest positive x that all days x, x + 1, ..., x + d_{i} - 1 are not scheduled for repair of any car. So, the car of the i-th client will be repaired in the range [x, x + d_{i} - 1]. It is possible that the day x when repair is scheduled to start will be less than s_{i}. 

Given n requests, you are asked to help Polycarp schedule all of them according to the rules above.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 200) — the number of requests from clients.

The following n lines contain requests, one request per line. The i-th request is given as the pair of integers s_{i}, d_{i} (1 ≤ s_{i} ≤ 10^9, 1 ≤ d_{i} ≤ 5·10^6), where s_{i} is the preferred time to start repairing the i-th car, d_{i} is the number of days to repair the i-th car.

The requests should be processed in the order they are given in the input.


-----Output-----

Print n lines. The i-th line should contain two integers — the start day to repair the i-th car and the finish day to repair the i-th car.


-----Examples-----
Input
3
9 2
7 3
2 4

Output
9 10
1 3
4 7

Input
4
1000000000 1000000
1000000000 1000000
100000000 1000000
1000000000 1000000

Output
1000000000 1000999999
1 1000000
100000000 100999999
1000001 2000000

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. We process requests in the order they come
4. For each request, we need to:
   - First try to schedule it starting at `s_i` (if available for `d_i` consecutive days)
   - If not available, find the earliest day `x` such that days `x` to `x+d_i-1` are all free
5. We need to output the actual start and finish days for each request

## Key Insights

1. We need to track which days are occupied by previous repairs
2. For each new request, we check if the preferred start day works
3. If not, we need to find the earliest possible slot that fits the duration requirement
4. Since constraints are large (up to 10^9 days), we can't store all days
5. Instead, we can store the intervals (start, end) of occupied days and manage them efficiently

## Algorithm

1. Maintain a list of occupied intervals (start, end) from previous requests
2. For each new request (s_i, d_i):
   - Check if the preferred interval [s_i, s_i + d_i - 1] is free
   - If free, schedule it there
   - If not free, find the earliest free consecutive block of d_i days
   - To find the earliest free block, we need to check gaps between existing intervals and at the beginning
3. When we schedule a new interval, add it to our list of occupied intervals

## Implementation Strategy

Since we only have at most 200 requests, we can maintain a list of intervals and check for overlap.

For each new request:
1. Create potential interval [s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]
2. Check if this interval overlaps with any existing interval
3. If no overlap, use it
4. If overlap, find the earliest free slot by checking gaps (between existing intervals and before the first interval)

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals of occupied days
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        end = s + d - 1
        
        # Check if preferred interval [s, end] is free
        is_free = True
        for start, end_interval in intervals:
            # Check if [s, end] overlaps with [start, end_interval]
            if not (end < start or s > end_interval):
                is_free = False
                break
        
        if is_free:
            # Schedule at preferred time
            start_day = s
            finish_day = end
        else:
            # Find the earliest free slot of duration d
            # Check gap before first interval
            if not intervals:
                start_day = 1
            else:
                # Check gaps between intervals and at beginning
                # First check if we can start at beginning
                start_day = 1
                # Check if there are gaps between existing intervals
                prev_end = 0
                found = False
                
                # Check gap before first interval
                if intervals and intervals[0][0] > 1:
                    # Gap from 1 to intervals[0][0]
                    gap_start = 1
                    gap_end = intervals[0][0] - 1
                    if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                        start_day = gap_start
                        found = True
                else:
                    # Check gaps between intervals
                    for i in range(len(intervals) - 1):
                        curr_end = intervals[i][1]
                        next_start = intervals[i + 1][0]
                        gap_start = curr_end + 1
                        gap_end = next_start - 1
                        if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                            start_day = gap_start
                            found = True
                            break
                
                # If not found in gaps, we need to extend intervals
                # But we should always be able to find a gap since we're adding new intervals
                if not found:
                    # This shouldn't happen, we can just extend to the end
                    # But we need to find the first gap
                    # Actually, we need to check all gaps systematically
                    start_day = 1
                    gap_found = False
                    
                    # Check all gaps
                    if intervals:
                        # Gap before first interval
                        if intervals[0][0] > 1:
                            gap_start = 1
                            gap_end = intervals[0][0] - 1
                            if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                                start_day = gap_start
                                gap_found = True
                        else:
                            # We can start at 1
                            start_day = 1

                            gap_start = curr_end + 1
                            gap_end = next_start - 1
                            if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                                start_day = gap_start
                                gap_found = True
                                break

                    if not gap_found:
                        # Find the earliest possible start
                        # This should always be possible - we can always extend
                        # But in practice, we need the first gap
                        start_day = 1
                        
                        # Recompute properly
                        first_gap = 1
                        for i in range(len(intervals)):
                            if intervals[i][0] > first_gap:
                                gap_start = first_gap
                                gap_end = intervals[i][0] - 1
                                if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                                    start_day = gap_start
                                    break
                            else:
                                # This interval starts before first_gap
                                # Check if we can start here
                                pass
                        
                        # If we didn't find, then we need to extend
                        # But since we're processing in order, gaps should exist
                        if start_day == 1:
                            # Find the actual first gap
                            gaps = []
                            gap_start = 1
                            for interval in intervals:
                                if gap_start < interval[0]:
                                    gaps.append((gap_start, interval[0] - 1))
                                gap_start = interval[1] + 1
                            if gaps:
                                for gap_start, gap_end in gaps:
                                    if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                                        start_day = gap_start
                                        break
                            else:
                                # No gaps found, extend to future
                                start_day = intervals[-1][1] + 1
                                if start_day + d - 1 <= 1000000000000:  # safe bound
                                    pass
                                else:

                        else:

                        start_day = 1
                        for interval in intervals:
                            if interval[0] > start_day:
                                gap_start = start_day
                                gap_end = interval[0] - 1
                                if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                                    start_day = gap_start
                                    break

                            if start_day + d - 1 <= 500000000000:  # safe bound
                                pass
                            else:
                                start_day = 1
                            
                        # We need to find the actual first gap
                        start_day = 1

                        for interval in sorted(intervals, key=lambda x: x[0]):
                            if current < interval[0]:
                                gap_start = current
                                gap_end = interval[0] - 1
                                if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                                    start_day = gap_start
                                    break
                            current = interval[1] + 1
                        if start_day == 1:
                            start_day = current
            finish_day = start_day + d - 1
        
        # Add the new interval to our list
        intervals.append((start_day, finish_day))
        
        # Output the result
        print(start_day, finish_day)

```python
n = int(input())
intervals = []

for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Check if preferred slot [s, s+d-1] is available
    available = True
    for start, end in intervals:
        # Check overlap between [s, s+d-1] and [start, end]
        if s <= end and s + d - 1 >= start:
            available = False
            break
    
    if available:
        start_day = s
        end_day = s + d - 1
    else:
        # Find the first gap of at least d days
        start_day = 1
        # First check if we can start at the beginning
        if intervals:
            # Check gap before first interval
            if intervals[0][0] > 1:
                gap_start = 1
                gap_end = intervals[0][0] - 1
                if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                    start_day = gap_start
            else:
                # First interval starts at or before 1, so check gaps between
                start_day = intervals[0][1] + 1
                # Check gaps between intervals
                for i in range(len(intervals) - 1):
                    curr_end = intervals[i][1]
                    next_start = intervals[i + 1][0]
                    gap_start = curr_end + 1
                    gap_end = next_start - 1
                    if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                        start_day = gap_start
                        break

```python
n = int(input())
intervals = []

for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Check if preferred slot [s, s+d-1] is available
    available = True
    for start, end in intervals:
        # Overlap if [s, s+d-1] and [start, end] have common days
        if s <= end and s + d - 1 >= start:
            available = False
            break
    
    if available:
        start_day = s
        end_day = s + d - 1
    else:
        # Find earliest available slot of d consecutive days
        start_day = 1
        end_day = 1 + d - 1

```python
n = int(input())
intervals = []  # List of (start, end) occupied periods

for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    preferred_start = s
    preferred_end = s + d - 1
    
    # Check if preferred slot is available
    available = True
    for start, end in intervals:
        # Check if there's overlap
        if not (preferred_end < start or preferred_start > end):
            available = False
            break
    
    if available:
        start_day = preferred_start
        end_day = preferred_end
    else:
        # Find the first available slot of duration d
        start_day = 1
        end_day = d - 1
        
        # Check all gaps in the occupied schedule
        # Gaps: before first, between intervals, after last
        
        # Start by checking the gap at the beginning
        if intervals:
            # Gap at beginning: from 1 to first interval's start - 1
            first_start = intervals[0][0]
            if first_start > 1:
                gap_start = 1
                gap_end = first_start - 1
                gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1
                if gap_length >= d:
                    start_day = gap_start
                    end_day = start_day + d - 1
                    continue  # Found a valid gap
        
        # Check gaps between intervals
        positions = [0]  # positions to check gaps before and between
        for i in range(len(intervals)):
            positions.append(intervals[i][1] + 1)
        
        for i in range(len(positions) - 1):
            gap_start = positions[i]
            gap_end = positions[i + 1] - 1
            gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1
            if gap_length >= d:
                start_day = gap_start
                end_day = gap_start + d - 1
                break
        else:
            # Gap after last interval
            start_day = intervals[-1][1] + 1
            end_day = start_day + d - 1
    
    intervals.append((start_day, end_day))

```python
n = int(input())
intervals = []  # (start, end) of occupied intervals

for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Check if preferred interval [s, s+d-1] is available
    is_available = True
    for start, end in intervals:
        if s <= end and s + d - 1 >= start:
            is_available = False
            break
    
    if is_available:
        start_day = s
        end_day = s + d - 1
    else:
        # Find the earliest free block of d consecutive days
        # Consider gaps: before first, between intervals, after last
        
        # We'll create a list of all gap boundaries
        gaps = []
        
        # Gap from 1 to first interval start (if first interval starts after 1)
        if intervals and intervals[0][0] > 1:
            gaps.append((1, intervals[0][0] - 1))
        
        # Gaps between intervals
        for i in range(len(intervals) - 1):
            curr_end = intervals[i][1]
            next_start = intervals[i + 1][0]
            if next_start > curr_end + 1:
                gaps.append((curr_end + 1, next_start - 1))
        
        # Gap after last interval
        if intervals:
            gaps.append((intervals[-1][1] + 1, float('inf')))

        start_day = 1
        end_day = d - 1
        found = False

            gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1
            if gap_length >= d:

View Extracted Code 
n = int(input())
intervals = []  # (start, end) of occupied intervals

for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Check if preferred interval [s, s+d-1] is available
    is_available = True
    for start, end in intervals:
        if s <= end and s + d - 1 >= start:
            is_available = False
            break
    
    if is_available:
        start_day = s
        end_day = s + d - 1
    else:
        # Find the earliest free block of d consecutive days
        # Consider gaps: before first, between intervals, after last
        
        # We'll create a list of all gap boundaries
        gaps = []
        
        # Gap from 1 to first interval start (if first interval starts after 1)
        if intervals and intervals[0][0] > 1:
            gaps.append((1, intervals[0][0] - 1))
        
        # Gaps between intervals
        for i in range(len(intervals) - 1):
            curr_end = intervals[i][1]
            next_start = intervals[i + 1][0]
            if next_start > curr_end + 1:
                gaps.append((curr_end + 1, next_start - 1))
        
        # Gap after last interval
        if intervals:
            gaps.append((intervals[-1][1] + 1, float('inf')))
        
        # Find first gap with at least d days
        start_day = 1
        end_day = d - 1
        found = False
        
        for gap_start, gap_end in gaps:
            gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1
            if gap_length >= d:
                start_day = gap_start
                end_day = gap_start + d - 1
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # This shouldn't happen, but just in case
            start_day = intervals[-1][1] + 1
            end_day = start_day + d - 1
    
    # Add the new occupied interval
    intervals.append((start_day, end_day))
    print(start_day, end_day)

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Polycarp starts his own business. Tomorrow will be the first working day of his car repair shop. For now the car repair shop is very small and only one car can be repaired at a given time.

Polycarp is good at marketing, so he has already collected n requests from clients. The requests are numbered from 1 to n in order they came.

The i-th request is characterized by two values: s_{i} — the day when a client wants to start the repair of his car, d_{i} — duration (in days) to repair the car. The days are enumerated from 1, the first day is tomorrow, the second day is the day after tomorrow and so on.

Polycarp is making schedule by processing requests in the order from the first to the n-th request. He schedules the i-th request as follows:  If the car repair shop is idle for d_{i} days starting from s_{i} (s_{i}, s_{i} + 1, ..., s_{i} + d_{i} - 1), then these days are used to repair a car of the i-th client.  Otherwise, Polycarp finds the first day x (from 1 and further) that there are d_{i} subsequent days when no repair is scheduled starting from x. In other words he chooses the smallest positive x that all days x, x + 1, ..., x + d_{i} - 1 are not scheduled for repair of any car. So, the car of the i-th client will be repaired in the range [x, x + d_{i} - 1]. It is possible that the day x when repair is scheduled to start will be less than s_{i}. 

Given n requests, you are asked to help Polycarp schedule all of them according to the rules above.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 200) — the number of requests from clients.

The following n lines contain requests, one request per line. The i-th request is given as the pair of integers s_{i}, d_{i} (1 ≤ s_{i} ≤ 10^9, 1 ≤ d_{i} ≤ 5·10^6), where s_{i} is the preferred time to start repairing the i-th car, d_{i} is the number of days to repair the i-th car.

The requests should be processed in the order they are given in the input.


-----Output-----

Print n lines. The i-th line should contain two integers — the start day to repair the i-th car and the finish day to repair the i-th car.


-----Examples-----

7 3
2 4

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request i has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. We process requests in order from 1 to n
4. For each request, we need to find the earliest possible start day:
   - First, check if the preferred period [s_i, s_i + d_i - 1] is free
   - If it's free, use it
   - If not, find the first available consecutive block of d_i days starting from some day x (where x ≥ 1)

## Key Insights

1. We need to keep track of all scheduled repair periods (intervals)
2. For each request, we need to check if a specific interval is free
3. If not free, we need to find the earliest available block of d_i consecutive days
4. The free days are determined by the union of all previously scheduled intervals

## Algorithm Steps

1. Maintain a list of active intervals (start, end) for all scheduled repairs
2. For each request:
   - Check if the preferred period [s_i, s_i + d_i - 1] is free by checking if it overlaps with any existing interval
   - If free, schedule it at preferred time
   - If not free, find the first gap where we can fit a block of d_i days
   - Add the new interval to our list

## Implementation Strategy

To find gaps efficiently:
- Sort the existing intervals by start day
- Check gaps between consecutive intervals
- Also check from day 1 onwards if there's a gap that fits

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        end = s + d - 1
        
        # Check if preferred period is available
        available = True
        # Check for overlap with existing intervals
        for interval_start, interval_end in intervals:
            if max(s, interval_start) <= min(end, interval_end):
                available = False
                break
        
        if available:
            # Preferred period is free, use it
            print(s, end)
            intervals.append((s, end))
        else:
            # Find first available block of d days
            # Check gaps between existing intervals
            # First, check if there's a gap from day 1
            found = False
            start_day = 1
            
            # Check if there's a gap before any existing interval
            if not intervals:
                start_day = 1
            else:
                # Check the gap before the first interval
                first_start, first_end = intervals[0]
                if first_start > 1:
                    # There's a gap from 1 to first_start - 1
                    if first_start - 1 >= d:
                        start_day = 1
                        found = True
                else:
                    # No gap before first interval, check gaps between intervals
                    for i in range(len(intervals) - 1):
                        curr_end = intervals[i][1]
                        next_start = intervals[i + 1][0]
                        gap_start = curr_end + 1
                        gap_end = next_start - 1
                        if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                            start_day = gap_start
                            found = True
                            break
                    # Check gap after last interval
                    if not found:
                        last_end = intervals[-1][1]
                        if (10**18 - last_end) >= d:  # sufficient gap
                            start_day = last_end + 1
                            found = True
            
            # If no gap found in the middle, use gap after last interval
            if not found:
                start_day = intervals[-1][1] + 1 if intervals else 1
            
            # Make sure we have enough days
            while True:
                # Check if start_day to start_day + d - 1 is free
                is_free = True
                for interval_start, interval_end in intervals:
                    if max(start_day, interval_start) <= min(start_day + d - 1, interval_end):
                        is_free = False
                        break
                
                if is_free:
                    break
                start_day += 1
            
            end_day = start_day + d - 1
            print(start_day, end_day)
            intervals.append((start_day, end_day))

This solution is simpler and should work. It checks each potential starting day from 1 upwards until it finds a free block of d consecutive days, which is the first available block we can use. The time complexity might be high if d is large and there are many intervals, but given the constraints (n ≤ 200), and that we're only checking gaps, it should be acceptable.

Let me verify with the examples:
- Example 1: (9,2), (7,3), (2,4)
  - First request: preferred [9,10] is free → use it
  - Second request: preferred [7,9] overlaps with [9,10] at day 9 → not free
    - Check day 1: [1,3] - doesn't overlap with [9,10] → free → use it
  - Third request: preferred [2,5] overlaps with [1,3] at days 2,3 → not free
    - Check day 4: [4,7] - doesn't overlap with [1,3] or [9,10] → free → use it

This matches the expected output.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. We process requests in the given order (from 1 to n)
4. For each request, we need to find a time slot of `d_i` consecutive days where no other repair is scheduled
5. If the preferred start day `s_i` is available (i.e., no repairs scheduled in days `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]`), we use that slot
6. Otherwise, we find the earliest start day `x` such that days `[x, x + d_i - 1]` are free
7. We output the start and finish days for each request

## Key Insights

1. We need to track all scheduled repair periods across all requests
2. For each new request, we need to check if the preferred start day is available
3. If not, we need to find the earliest available gap of size `d_i`
4. Since we process requests in order, we can maintain a list of all current scheduled intervals

## Algorithm Steps

1. Maintain a list of intervals (start, end) representing scheduled repair times
2. For each request (s_i, d_i):
   - Check if the preferred interval [s_i, s_i + d_i - 1] is available
   - If available, use it
   - If not available, find the earliest available gap of size d_i
3. When adding a new interval, merge overlapping intervals to maintain a clean schedule

## Implementation Strategy

1. Store all scheduled intervals as (start, end)
2. For each request, check if the preferred period overlaps with existing intervals
3. If no overlap, use the preferred period
4. If overlap, find the first gap of size d_i

To find gaps efficiently, I'll sort the intervals by start time and merge overlapping ones.

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        start = s
        end = s + d - 1
        
        # Check if the preferred interval is available
        available = True
        for interval_start, interval_end in intervals:
            # Check if there's overlap
            if interval_start <= end and start <= interval_end:
                available = False
                break
        
        if available:
            # Preferred slot is available
            result_start = start
            result_end = end
        else:
            # Find the first free gap of size d
            # First, merge all existing intervals to get gaps
            merged = []
            if intervals:
                # Sort intervals by start
                sorted_intervals = sorted(intervals)
                current_start, current_end = sorted_intervals[0]
                for interval_start, interval_end in sorted_intervals[1:]:
                    if interval_start <= current_end + 1:
                        # Overlapping or adjacent, merge
                        current_end = max(current_end, interval_end)
                    else:
                        # Add current merged interval and start new one
                        merged.append((current_start, current_end))
                        current_start, current_end = interval_start, interval_end
                merged.append((current_start, current_end))
            
            # Now find the earliest gap of size d
            # Check gaps between intervals
            gaps = []
            for i in range(len(merged) - 1):
                gap_start = merged[i][1] + 1
                gap_end = merged[i+1][0] - 1
                if gap_start <= gap_end:
                    gaps.append((gap_start, gap_end))
            
            # Also check before first interval and after last interval
            if merged and merged[0][0] > 1:
                gaps.append((1, merged[0][0] - 1))
            if merged and merged[-1][1] < 10**18:
                gaps.append((merged[-1][1] + 1, 10**18))
            
            # Find the smallest gap that can fit d days
            result_start = float('inf')
            for gap_start, gap_end in gaps:
                if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                    result_start = gap_start
                    break
            
            # If no gap found, use the first available day (should never happen)
            if result_start == float('inf'):
                result_start = 1
                result_end = d - 1
        
        # Add the new interval
        intervals.append((result_start, result_end))
        
        # Output result
        print(result_start, result_end)

solve()
```

Wait, I need to fix this. The gap finding logic is getting complex. Let me simplify:

```python
n = int(input())
intervals = []  # list of (start, end) intervals

for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    end = s + d - 1
    
    # Check if preferred interval [s, end] is available
    available = True
    for start, end_interval in intervals:
        if start <= end and s <= end_interval:
            available = False
            break
    
    if available:
        result_start, result_end = s, end
    else:
        # Find the first available gap of size d
        # First, create a list of all current intervals and sort by start
        current_intervals = [(start, end_interval) for start, end_interval in intervals]
        current_intervals.sort()
        
        # Merge overlapping intervals
        merged = []
        if current_intervals:
            current_start, current_end = current_intervals[0]
            for next_start, next_end in current_intervals[1:]:
                if next_start <= current_end + 1:
                    current_end = max(current_end, next_end)
                else:
                    merged.append((current_start, current_end))
                    current_start, current_end = next_start, next_end
            merged.append((current_start, current_end))
        
        # Find gaps between merged intervals
        result_start = 1
        for i in range(len(merged)):
            if i == 0:
                # Gap before first interval
                gap_start = 1
                gap_end = merged[0][0] - 1
                if gap_end >= gap_start and gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                    result_start = gap_start
                    break
            else:
                # Gap between intervals
                gap_start = merged[i-1][1] + 1
                gap_end = merged[i][0] - 1
                if gap_end >= gap_start and gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                    result_start = gap_start
                    break
        
        # If we didn't find a gap in the middle, check after the last interval
        if result_start == 1:
            gap_start = merged[-1][1] + 1
            gap_end = 10**18
            if gap_end - gap_start + 1 >= d:
                result_start = gap_start
            else:
                result_start = 1  # fallback
        
        result_end = result_start + d - 1
    
    intervals.append((result_start, result_end))
    print(result_start, result_end)

So, the car of the i-th client will be repaired in the range [x, x + d_{i} - 1]. It is possible that the day x when repair is scheduled to start will be less than s_{i}. 

Given n requests, you are asked to help Polycarp schedule all of them according to the rules above.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 200) — the number of requests from clients.

The following n lines contain requests, one request per line. The i-th request is given as the pair of integers s_{i}, d_{i} (1 ≤ s_{i} ≤ 10^9, 1 ≤ d_{i} ≤ 5·10^6), where s_{i} is the preferred time to start repairing the i-th car, d_{i} is the number of days to repair the i-th car.

The requests should be processed in the order they are given in the input.


-----Output-----

Print n lines. The i-th line should contain two integers — the start day to repair the i-th car and the finish day to repair the i-th car.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. For each request, we need to find a time interval of `d_i` consecutive days where no other repair is scheduled
4. If the preferred start day is available (no conflicts), we use it
5. Otherwise, we find the earliest available time slot of `d_i` consecutive days
6. We output the start and end days for each repair

## Key Insights

1. We need to track all scheduled repair intervals to check for conflicts
2. For each request, we first check if the preferred start day works
3. If it doesn't work, we need to find the first available block of `d_i` consecutive days
4. We need to efficiently find the earliest available block

## Algorithm Steps

1. Keep track of all currently scheduled intervals
2. For each request:
   - Check if the preferred start day `s_i` works by verifying if all days `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]` are free
   - If yes, schedule at preferred start
   - If no, find the earliest available block of `d_i` consecutive days by scanning from day 1 onwards
   - Add the scheduled interval to our list of occupied days

## Implementation Strategy

Since we need to find free intervals efficiently, we can use a list of intervals that are already scheduled, and check for overlaps.

For each new request:
1. Check if preferred start works by testing if there's any existing interval that overlaps with `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]`
2. If no overlap, use the preferred start
3. Otherwise, find the first free block of `d_i` days by scanning from day 1 until we find a gap

```python
n = int(input())
requests = []
for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    requests.append((s, d))

# Store scheduled intervals as (start, end)
scheduled = []

for s, d in requests:
    # First, try to use the preferred start day
    preferred_start = s
    preferred_end = s + d - 1
    
    # Check if preferred start works - no overlap with existing scheduled intervals
    valid_preferred = True
    for start, end in scheduled:
        # Check if there's overlap between current preferred interval and existing interval
        if not (preferred_end < start or preferred_start > end):
            valid_preferred = False
            break
    
    if valid_preferred:
        # Use preferred start
        start_day = preferred_start
        end_day = preferred_end
    else:
        # Need to find the first available block of d consecutive days
        start_day = 1
        end_day = start_day + d - 1
        
        # Keep searching until we find a valid block
        while True:
            # Check if the block [start_day, end_day] has any conflicts
            valid_block = True
            for existing_start, existing_end in scheduled:
                # Check for overlap
                if not (end_day < existing_start or start_day > existing_end):
                    valid_block = False
                    break
            
            if valid_block:
                break
            
            start_day += 1
            end_day = start_day + d - 1
    
    # Add this new interval to scheduled
    scheduled.append((start_day, end_day))
    
    # Output the result for this request
    print(start_day, end_day)
```

Actually, let me think about this differently. The key insight is that we need to find the first gap of at least `d` consecutive days.

Let me optimize by efficiently finding the first gap:

```python
n = int(input())
requests = []
for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    requests.append((s, d))

scheduled = []  # list of (start, end) intervals

for s, d in requests:
    # Try preferred start first
    preferred_start = s
    preferred_end = s + d - 1
    
    # Check if preferred start interval conflicts with any existing schedule
    conflicts = False
    for start, end in scheduled:
        if start <= preferred_end and preferred_start <= end:
            conflicts = True
            break
    
    if not conflicts:
        # Use preferred start
        start_day = preferred_start
        end_day = preferred_end
    else:
        # Find the first available block of d consecutive days
        start_day = 1
        while True:
            end_day = start_day + d - 1
            # Check if there's any conflict in the interval [start_day, end_day]
            valid = True
            for ex_start, ex_end in scheduled:
                # Check if there's overlap
                if ex_start <= end_day and start_day <= ex_end:
                    valid = False
                    break
            
            if valid:
                break
            start_day += 1
    
    # Add the new interval to scheduled list
    scheduled.append((start_day, end_day))
    
    print(start_day, end_day)

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Polycarp starts his own business. Tomorrow will be the first working day of his car repair shop. For now the car repair shop is very small and only one car can be repaired at a given time.

Polycarp is good at marketing, so he has already collected n requests from clients. The requests are numbered from 1 to n in order they came.

Otherwise, Polycarp finds the first day x (from 1 and further) that there are d_{i} subsequent days when no repair is scheduled starting from x. In other words he chooses the smallest positive x that all days x, x + 1, ..., x + d_{i} - 1 are not scheduled for repair of any car. So, the car of the i-th client will be repaired in the range [x, x + d_{i} - 1]. It is possible that the day x when repair is scheduled to start will be less than s_{i}. 

Given n requests, you are asked to help Polycarp schedule all of them according to the rules above.

The requests should be processed in the order they are given in the input.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled in order
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. We process requests in input order (from 1 to n)
4. For each request, we need to:
   - First check if the preferred period `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]` is free
   - If it's free, use that period
   - If it's not free, find the earliest possible start day `x` such that `[x, x + d_i - 1]` is completely free

## Key Insights

1. We need to track which days are already occupied by previous repairs
2. The constraint is that we need `d_i` consecutive free days starting from day `x`
3. We process requests sequentially, so we only need to consider conflicts with previously scheduled repairs
4. For each new request, we need to find the first gap of size `d_i` in the timeline

## Algorithm

1. Keep track of all scheduled repair intervals (start, end)
2. For each request:
   - Check if the preferred period `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]` is available
   - If available, schedule it there
   - If not available, find the first gap of size `d_i` and schedule there
3. To find the gap, we need to merge overlapping intervals and find the earliest available gap of sufficient size

## Implementation Strategy

1. Store all scheduled intervals
2. For each new request, check if the preferred period conflicts with any existing interval
3. If no conflict, use the preferred period
4. If conflict, find the first free gap of size `d_i`

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    intervals = []  # list of (start, end) for scheduled repairs
    
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        preferred_start = s
        duration = d
        
        # Check if preferred period is available
        preferred_end = preferred_start + duration - 1
        
        # Check if preferred period conflicts with any existing interval
        conflict = False
        conflict_start = -1
        
        for start, end in intervals:
            # Check for overlap between preferred period and existing interval
            if start <= preferred_end and preferred_start <= end:
                conflict = True
                break
        
        if not conflict:
            # Preferred period is available
            intervals.append((preferred_start, preferred_end))
            print(preferred_start, preferred_end)
        else:
            # Find the first gap of size duration
            # First, merge all intervals to get non-overlapping gaps
            temp_intervals = intervals[:]
            temp_intervals.sort()
            
            # Merge overlapping intervals
            merged = []
            for start, end in temp_intervals:
                if not merged:
                    merged.append([start, end])
                else:
                    last = merged[-1]
                    if start <= last[1]:
                        # Overlapping, merge
                        merged[-1][1] = max(last[1], end)
                    else:
                        merged.append([start, end])
            
            # Find the first gap of size duration
            gaps = []
            # Gap before first interval
            if merged and merged[0][0] > 1:
                gaps.append((1, merged[0][0] - 1))
            
            # Gaps between intervals
            for i in range(len(merged) - 1):
                gaps.append((merged[i][1] + 1, merged[i + 1][0] - 1))
            
            # Gap after last interval
            if merged and merged[-1][1] + 1 > 1:
                gaps.append((merged[-1][1] + 1, float('inf')))
            
            # Find the first gap that has at least duration days
            start_day = 1
            for gap_start, gap_end in gaps:
                gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1
                if gap_length >= duration:
                    start_day = gap_start
                    break
            
            # If no gap found or all gaps are too small, start from the first day
            if start_day == 1:
                start_day = 1
            
            # Schedule in the found gap
            actual_start = start_day
            actual_end = actual_start + duration - 1
            
            # Add to intervals
            intervals.append((actual_start, actual_end))
            print(actual_start, actual_end)

solve()

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have n car repair requests that need to be scheduled
2. Each request has a preferred start day `s_i` and duration `d_i`
3. We process requests in order from 1 to n
4. For each request, we need to find a contiguous block of `d_i` days for repair
5. If the preferred period `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]` is available (no conflicts), we use it
6. Otherwise, we find the earliest possible day `x` such that `[x, x + d_i - 1]` is completely free
7. We output the start and finish days for each request

## Key Insights

1. We need to track all scheduled repair periods to check availability
2. For each new request, we first check if the preferred period is available
3. If not available, we need to find the first available consecutive block of `d_i` days
4. We need to efficiently check intervals and find gaps

## Algorithm

1. Maintain a list of all currently scheduled repair periods (as intervals)
2. For each request:
   - Check if the preferred period `[s_i, s_i + d_i - 1]` is available
   - If available, use it
   - If not available, find the first gap of at least `d_i` consecutive days
3. When placing a new interval, insert it into our list of intervals

## Implementation Strategy

1. Use a list to store scheduled intervals
2. For each request, check if preferred period overlaps with any existing interval
3. If no overlap, use preferred period
4. If overlap, find the first available gap of sufficient length
5. Place the new interval in the proper position

```python
def solve():
    n = int(input())
    requests = []
    for _ in range(n):
        s, d = map(int, input().split())
        requests.append((s, d))
    
    # List of scheduled intervals (start, end)
    scheduled = []
    
    for s, d in requests:
        preferred_start = s
        preferred_end = s + d - 1
        
        # Check if preferred period is available
        is_available = True
        for start, end in scheduled:
            # Check if preferred period overlaps with existing interval
            if not (preferred_end < start or preferred_start > end):
                is_available = False
                break
        
        if is_available:
            # Preferred period is available, use it
            start_day = preferred_start
            end_day = preferred_end
        else:
            # Find the first available gap of d_i consecutive days
            # We need to find the earliest day x such that [x, x+d-1] has no overlap
            # with any existing interval
            
            # First, find all gaps between existing intervals
            gaps = []
            
            # Gap before the first interval
            if not scheduled:
                gaps.append((1, 0))  # (start, length) - start at day 1, length 0 means no gap
            
            # Gaps between intervals
            for i in range(len(scheduled) - 1):
                current_end = scheduled[i][1]
                next_start = scheduled[i + 1][0]
                gap_start = current_end + 1
                gap_end = next_start - 1
                if gap_start <= gap_end:
                    gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1
                    if gap_length >= d:
                        gaps.append((gap_start, gap_length))
            
            # Gap after the last interval
            if scheduled:
                last_end = scheduled[-1][1]
                # Gap from last_end + 1 to infinity
                gap_start = last_end + 1
                gap_length = float('inf')
                if gap_length >= d:
                    gaps.append((gap_start, gap_length))
            
            # If we found gaps with sufficient length, choose the earliest one
            if gaps:
                # Sort gaps by start day
                gaps.sort()
                # Choose the gap with the smallest start day
                gap_start, gap_length = gaps[0]
                start_day = gap_start
                end_day = gap_start + d - 1
            else:
                # This should not happen given the constraints
                # We need to find the first day where d days are free
                # Actually, we must find the next available day
                # Let's extend our gap finding to find the first free block
                
                # Find the first day where we have d consecutive free days
                # We'll check from day 1 onwards
                start_day = 1
                end_day = start_day + d - 1
                
                # Check if this block overlaps with any existing interval
                overlap_found = False
                for s_int, e_int in scheduled:
                    if not (end_day < s_int or start_day > e_int):
                        overlap_found = True
                        break
                
                if overlap_found:
                    # We need to find the next available position
                    # Keep incrementing start_day until we find a free block
                    while True:
                        start_day += 1
                        end_day = start_day + d - 1
                        overlap_found = False
                        for s_int, e_int in scheduled:
                            if not (end_day < s_int or start_day > e_int):
                                overlap_found = True
                                break
                        if not overlap_found:
                            break
                else:
                    pass  # already found a free block
            
            # Wait, the gap finding above might not be correct
            # Let's implement a simpler but correct gap finding
            
            # Actually, let's implement a more straightforward method
            # We'll find all gaps and pick the first one that can fit d days
            
            # Clear the gaps list and recalculate
            gaps = []
            
            # Gap before first interval
            if not scheduled:
                # We can start at day 1
                gaps.append((1, 0))
            else:
                first_start = scheduled[0][0]
                gaps.append((1, first_start - 1))
            
            # Gaps between intervals
            for i in range(len(scheduled) - 1):
                current_end = scheduled[i][1]
                next_start = scheduled[i + 1][0]
                gap_start = current_end + 1
                gap_end = next_start - 1
                gap_length = gap_end - gap_start + 1 if gap_end >= gap_start else 0
                if gap_length >= d:
                    gaps.append((gap_start, gap_length))
            
            # Gap after last interval
            if scheduled:
                last_end = scheduled[-1][1]
                # Gap from last_end + 1 to infinity
                gap_start = last_end + 1
                # This gap has infinite length, so it's definitely long enough
                gaps.append((gap_start, float('inf')))
            
            # If we have gaps with at least d days, pick the first one
            if gaps:
                # Sort by start day
                gaps.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
                gap_start, gap_length = gaps[0]
                start_day = gap_start
                end_day = gap_start + d - 1
            else:
                # This should not happen, but let's find the first day
                start_day = 1
                end_day = start_day + d - 1
                # Keep incrementing until we find a free block
                while True:
                    # Check if [start_day, start_day + d - 1] overlaps with any scheduled interval
                    can_place = True
                    for s_int, e_int in scheduled:
                        if not (end_day < s_int or start_day > e_int):
                            can_place = False
                            break
                    if can_place:
                        break
                    start_day += 1
                    end_day = start_day + d - 1
        
        # Add the new interval to scheduled list
        scheduled.append((start_day, end_day))
        
        print(start_day, end_day)

solve()

```python
n = int(input())
requests = []
for _ in range(n):
    s, d = map(int, input().split())
    requests.append((s, d))

# List of scheduled intervals (start, end)
scheduled = []

for s, d in requests:
    preferred_start = s
    preferred_end = s + d - 1
    
    # Check if preferred period is available
    is_available = True
    for start, end in scheduled:
        # Check for overlap: if preferred period overlaps with existing interval
        if not (preferred_end < start or preferred_start > end):
            is_available = False
            break
    
    if is_available:
        start_day = preferred_start
        end_day = preferred_end
    else:
        # Find the first gap of d consecutive days
        # We'll check all possible gaps: before first, between intervals, after last
        
        # Start with the earliest possible day
        start_day = 1
        end_day = start_day + d - 1
        
        # Keep trying to find a free block
        while True:
            # Check if the current block [start_day, end_day] overlaps with any scheduled interval
            valid = True
            for s_int, e_int in scheduled:
                if not (end_day < s_int or start_day > e_int):
                    valid = False
                    break
            
            if valid:
                break
                
            start_day += 1
            end_day = start_day + d - 1
    
    # Add the new interval
    scheduled.append((start_day, end_day))

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Codefortia is a small island country located somewhere in the West Pacific. It consists of n settlements connected by m bidirectional gravel roads. Curiously enough, the beliefs of the inhabitants require the time needed to pass each road to be equal either to a or b seconds. It's guaranteed that one can go between any pair of settlements by following a sequence of roads.

Codefortia was recently struck by the financial crisis. Therefore, the king decided to abandon some of the roads so that:

  * it will be possible to travel between each pair of cities using the remaining roads only, 
  * the sum of times required to pass each remaining road will be minimum possible (in other words, remaining roads must form minimum spanning tree, using the time to pass the road as its weight), 
  * among all the plans minimizing the sum of times above, the time required to travel between the king's residence (in settlement 1) and the parliament house (in settlement p) using the remaining roads only will be minimum possible. 



The king, however, forgot where the parliament house was. For each settlement p = 1, 2, ..., n, can you tell what is the minimum time required to travel between the king's residence and the parliament house (located in settlement p) after some roads are abandoned?

Input

The first line of the input contains four integers n, m, a and b (2 ≤ n ≤ 70, n - 1 ≤ m ≤ 200, 1 ≤ a < b ≤ 10^7) — the number of settlements and gravel roads in Codefortia, and two possible travel times. Each of the following lines contains three integers u, v, c (1 ≤ u, v ≤ n, u ≠ v, c ∈ \\{a, b\}) denoting a single gravel road between the settlements u and v, which requires c minutes to travel.

You can assume that the road network is connected and has no loops or multiedges.

Output

Output a single line containing n integers. The p-th of them should denote the minimum possible time required to travel from 1 to p after the selected roads are abandoned. Note that for each p you can abandon a different set of roads.

Examples

Input


5 5 20 25
1 2 25
2 3 25
3 4 20
4 5 20
5 1 20


Output


0 25 60 40 20


Input


6 7 13 22
1 2 13
2 3 13
1 4 22
3 4 13
4 5 13
5 6 13
6 1 13


Output


0 13 26 39 26 13

Note

The minimum possible sum of times required to pass each road in the first example is 85 — exactly one of the roads with passing time 25 must be abandoned. Note that after one of these roads is abandoned, it's now impossible to travel between settlements 1 and 3 in time 50.

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` settlements connected by `m` bidirectional roads
2. Each road takes either `a` or `b` seconds to traverse (with `a < b`)
3. We need to abandon some roads to form a minimum spanning tree (MST)
4. Among all MSTs with minimum total weight, for each settlement `p`, we want the minimum time from settlement 1 to p
5. We need to output the shortest path from settlement 1 to each settlement p

## Key Insights

1. Since all edge weights are either `a` or `b`, and we want minimum spanning tree, we should prefer edges with weight `a` over edges with weight `b`
2. The MST will consist of the minimum number of edges needed to connect all vertices, with priority given to `a`-weighted edges
3. After forming the MST, we need to find shortest paths from vertex 1 to all other vertices

## Algorithm Steps

1. First, identify all edges and separate them by weight
2. Build the MST by prioritizing `a`-weighted edges, then `b`-weighted edges
3. For each possible destination `p`, we need to find the shortest path in the MST from 1 to p
4. Since we can abandon different sets of roads for different destinations, we need to consider all possible MSTs and find the one that minimizes the path from 1 to p

Wait, let me reconsider the problem. Actually, we need to choose an MST that minimizes the total weight, and among those, we want to minimize the distance from 1 to p for each p.

Since all edge weights are either a or b, and we want minimum total weight, we should:
- Use as many a-weighted edges as possible
- Use b-weighted edges only when necessary

## Approach

1. First, create a graph with all edges
2. Identify which edges belong to the MST by preferring a-weighted edges
3. For each destination p, we need to find the shortest path in the MST from 1 to p

However, since we have multiple MSTs (because some edges with weight a might be replaced by edges with weight b if they create cycles), we need to be careful.

Actually, since we want minimum total weight, we should use exclusively a-weighted edges whenever possible. The MST will use the minimum required number of b-weighted edges to connect all components.

Let me build the MST by first including all a-weighted edges, then add b-weighted edges as needed.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Group edges by weight
2. First, add all a-weighted edges (they are cheaper)
3. Then add b-weighted edges that are necessary to connect components (using union-find)
4. After building the MST, run Dijkstra or BFS to find shortest paths from 1 to all other vertices

```python
def find_parent(parent, x):
    if parent[x] != x:
        parent[x] = find_parent(parent, parent[x])
    return parent[x]

def union(parent, rank, x, y):
    px, py = find_parent(parent, x), find_parent(parent, y)
    if px == py:
        return False
    if rank[px] < rank[py]:
        px, py = py, px
    parent[py] = px
    if rank[px] == rank[py]:
        rank[px] += 1
    return True

# Read input
n, m, a, b = map(int, input().split())

edges = []
for _ in range(m):
    u, v, c = map(int, input().split())
    edges.append((c, u, v))

# Separate edges by weight
a_edges = []
b_edges = []

for c, u, v in edges:
    if c == a:
        a_edges.append((u, v))
    else:  # c == b
        b_edges.append((u, v))

# Build MST using all a-edges first, then b-edges as needed
parent = list(range(n + 1))
rank = [0] * (n + 1)

# First, add all a-weighted edges
for u, v in a_edges:
    union(parent, rank, u, v)

# Then, add b-weighted edges that connect different components
mst_edges = []
b_edge_idx = 0

# For each b-edge, if it connects two different components, add it
for u, v in b_edges:
    if find_parent(parent, u) != find_parent(parent, v):
        union(parent, rank, u, v)
        mst_edges.append((u, v))

# Now build the adjacency list for the MST
adj = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
for u, v in mst_edges:
    adj[u].append(v)
    adj[v].append(u)

# Find shortest path from 1 to all other nodes using BFS (since all weights are equal)
# But wait: all edges in MST have the same weight (a or b). However, we need to handle the fact that 
# we might have different path costs due to different edge weights.

# Actually, I need to reconsider. The MST has edges of weight a and b. When calculating path from 1 to p,
# we need to find the path that minimizes the total time, which means we prefer paths with more edges of weight a.

# But since we've constructed the MST with minimum total weight, and we want minimum path from 1 to p,
# we should use Dijkstra or BFS where edge weights are properly accounted for.

# Create a properly weighted graph
weighted_adj = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
for u, v in mst_edges:
    # We know the weight of this edge, but in our construction we don't know which one it is
    # So we need to reconstruct the actual weights
    pass

# Let me reconstruct the MST with actual weights
# I need to store the weight of each edge, not just the endpoints

# Recreate the MST representation with weights
mst_edges_with_weight = []

# Rebuild MST, tracking weights
parent = list(range(n + 1))
rank = [0] * (n + 1)

# Process a-edges first
for u, v in a_edges:
    if find_parent(parent, u) != find_parent(parent, v):
        union(parent, rank, u, v)
        mst_edges_with_weight.append((u, v, a))

# Process b-edges
for u, v in b_edges:
    if find_parent(parent, u) != find_parent(parent, v):
        union(parent, rank, u, v)
        mst_edges_with_weight.append((u, v, b))

# Build adjacency list with weights
adj = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
for u, v, w in mst_edges_with_weight:
    adj[u].append((v, w))
    adj[v].append((u, w))

# Now do Dijkstra to find shortest path from 1 to all nodes
import heapq

def dijkstra(start, adj, n):
    dist = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    dist[start] = 0
    pq = [(0, start)]
    
    while pq:
        d, u = heapq.heappop(pq)
        if d > dist[u]:
            continue
        for v, w in adj[u]:
            new_dist = d + w
            if new_dist < dist[v]:
                dist[v] = new_dist
                heapq.heappush(pq, (new_dist, v))
    
    return dist

# For each destination p, find the shortest path from 1 to p
result = []
for p in range(1, n + 1):
    dist = dijkstra(1, adj, n)
    result.append(str(dist[p]))

print(' '.join(result))
